. Since

s_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}\,X}^{\bullet}(F)
is convex and
\mathbb{P}f
commutes with
scalar multiplication and with addition,
A
is convex. By
Fact
13.1
,
cl(A)
is convex, too. ∎

We can now transport Theorem
13.5
to the world of
sublinear previsions, as follows.

Theorem 13.8

Let
{(p_{ij}\colon X_{j}\to X_{i})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
be a
projective system of topological spaces, with canonical projective
limit
X,{(p_{i})}_{i\in I}
. Let
\bullet
be nothing,
“
\leq 1
” or “
1
”.
If:

1.

the projective system is an ep-system,

2.

or
I
has a countable cofinal subset and each
X_{i}
is
locally compact sober (and compact, if
\bullet
is “
1
”),

3.

or every
X_{i}
is
\odot
-consonant sober (and compact if

\bullet
is “
1
”) and every
p_{ij}
is a proper map,

then

{(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
is a projective
system of topological spaces, and

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X,{(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{i}})}_{i\in I}
is its
projective limit, up to homeomorphism.

Proof 56

In case 1, we use Proposition
10.4
, noticing that

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
is a subdcpo of
KX
, namely that pointwise
directed suprema of (subnormalized, normalized) sublinear previsions
are again (subnormalized, normalized) sublinear previsions.

In cases 2 and 3, we apply the corresponding cases of
Theorem
13.5
. To this end, we need to verify that

r_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}
and
s^{\bullet}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}
are a natural homeomorphism on a
subcategory of
\mathbf{Top}
that contains the spaces
X_{i}
and the
limit
X
; this is a special case of
Lemma
11.2
where the retraction is in fact a
homeomorphism. In light of Lemma
13.7
, it suffices
to show that every
X_{i}
is
{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\bullet}
-friendly, as well as
X
.

In case 2, every locally compact sober space is

{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\bullet}
-friendly, and that is the case of each
X_{i}
.
X
may
fail to be locally compact, but it is
\odot
-consonant by
Proposition
12.17
. When
\bullet
is
“
1
”, it is also compact by Steenrod’s theorem. In any case,
X

is
{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\bullet}
-friendly by Fact
13.6
.

In case 3, every
p_{ij}
is proper, and every
X_{i}
is

\odot
-consonant sober, so
X
is, too, by
Corollary
12.15
. When
\bullet
is “
1
”,
every
X_{i}
is compact sober, so
X
is, too, by Steenrod’s theorem.
By Fact
13.6
, all the spaces and
X_{i}
and
X
are
therefore
{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}_{\bullet}
-friendly.

14
Forks

We arrive at our final functors, which mix probabilistic and erratic
non-determinism. A
fork
on a space
X
is any pair

(F^{-},F^{+})
of a superlinear prevision
F^{-}
on
X
and of a
sublinear prevision
F^{+}
on
X
satisfying
Walley’s condition
:

F^{-}(h+h^{\prime})\leq F^{-}(h)+F^{+}(h^{\prime})\leq F^{+}(h+h^{\prime})

for all
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}X

[
15
,
37
]
. A
fork is
subnormalized
, resp.
normalized
if and only if
both
F^{-}
and
F^{+}
are.

We write
\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}}X
for the set of all forks on
X
, and

\mathbb{P}^{\leq 1}_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}}X
,
\mathbb{P}^{1}_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}}X
for their subsets of
subnormalized, resp. normalized, forks. The
weak topology
on
each is the subspace topology induced by the inclusion into the larger
space
\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}}X\times\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}}X
. A subbase of the weak
topology is composed of two kinds of open subsets:
[h>r]^{-}
,
defined as
\{(F^{-},F^{+})\mid F^{-}(h)>r\}
, and
[h>r]^{+}
, defined
as
\{(F^{-},F^{+})\mid F^{+}(h)>r\}
, where
h\in\mathcal{L}X
,

r\in\mathbb{R}^{+}
. The specialization ordering of spaces of forks is
the product ordering
\leq\times\leq
, where
\leq
denotes the
pointwise ordering on previsions. In particular, all those spaces of
forks are
T_{0}
.

It is easy to see that, whether
\bullet
is nothing, “
\leq 1
”, or
“
1
”,
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
defines an endofunctor on
\mathbf{Top}
,
whose action on morphisms is given by

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}f\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}(\mathbb{P}f,\mathbb{P}f)
.

Lemma 14.1

Let
\bullet
be nothing, “
\leq 1
”, or “
1
”, and
T
be the

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
functor. The comparison map

\varphi\colon TX\to Z
of any projective
T
-situation is a
topological embedding.

Proof 57

Let
Z^{\sharp},{(q_{i}^{\sharp})}_{i\in I}
be the canonical projective
limit of

(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
and

\varphi^{\sharp}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X\to Z^{\sharp}
be the
comparison map. Similarly with
Z^{\flat},{(q_{i}^{\flat})}_{i\in I}

and
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
. We also take the notations (
Z
,

\varphi
,
q_{i}
) from Definition
3.1
, with

T\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
.

By definition (see Definition
3.1
),

\varphi^{\sharp}
maps every
F^{-}\in\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
to

{(\mathbb{P}p_{i}(F^{-}))}_{i\in I}
,
\varphi^{\flat}
maps every

F^{+}\in\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
to
{(\mathbb{P}p_{i}(F^{+}))}_{i\in I}
,
and
\varphi
maps every fork
(F^{-},F^{+})\in\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X

to
{(\mathbb{P}p_{i}(F^{-}),\mathbb{P}p_{i}(F^{+}))}_{i\in I}
. Also, the maps

q_{i}^{\sharp}
,
q_{i}^{\flat}
,
q_{i}
are just projection onto coordinate

i
, just like
p_{i}
.

As a consequence, for every
i\in I
, for every

h_{i}\in\mathcal{L}{X_{i}}
, for every
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, for every

\pm\in\{-,+\}
,

{\varphi}^{-1}(q_{i}^{-1}([h_{i}>r]^{\pm}))=[h_{i}\circ p_{i}]^{\pm}
.
Indeed,
(F^{-},F^{+})
is in the left-hand side if and only if

\mathbb{P}{p_{i}}(F^{\pm})(h_{i})>r
, if and only if

F^{\pm}(h_{i}\circ p_{i})>r
, if and only if

(F^{-},F^{+})\in[h_{i}\circ p_{i}>r]^{\pm}
.

A subbase of the topology on
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
is given by the
sets
[h>r]^{\pm}
where
h\in\mathcal{L}X
,
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, and

\pm\in\{+,-\}
. For every
i\in I
, let
h_{i}
be the largest
map in
\mathcal{L}{X_{i}}
such that
h_{i}\circ p_{i}\leq h
, as given in
Lemma
10.2
. Now
[h>r]^{\pm}
is the collection of
(subnormalized, normalized) forks
(F^{-},F^{+})
such that

F^{\pm}(h)>r
, or equivalently such that

F^{\pm}(h_{i}\circ p_{i})>r
for some
i\in I
, using item 6 of that
lemma and the Scott-continuity of
F^{\pm}
. In other words,

[h>r]^{\pm}=\bigcup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}[h_{i}\circ p_{i}>r]^{\pm}
, and we have
seen that this is equal to

\bigcup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}{\varphi}^{-1}(q_{i}^{-1}([h_{i}>r]^{\pm}))
, hence
to
{\varphi}^{-1}(\bigcup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}q_{i}^{-1}([h_{i}>r]^{\pm}))
.
Therefore
\varphi
is full.

Since
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
is
T_{0}
,
\varphi
is a topological
embedding. ∎

Theorem 14.2

Let
\bullet
be nothing, “
\leq 1
”, or “
1
”. Let

{(p_{ij}\colon X_{j}\to X_{i})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
be a
projective system of topological spaces, with canonical projective
limit
X,{(p_{i})}_{i\in I}
. If
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
is a projective limit of

(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
and if

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
is a projective limit of

{(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
, then

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
is a projective limit of

(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
.

Proof 58

Let
Z^{\sharp},{(q_{i}^{\sharp})}_{i\in I}
be the canonical projective
limit of

(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
and

\varphi^{\sharp}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X\to Z^{\sharp}
be the
comparison map. Similarly with
Z^{\flat},{(q_{i}^{\flat})}_{i\in I}

and
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
, with

Z^{\natural},{(q_{i}^{\natural})}_{i\in I}
and
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
.
By assumption,
\varphi^{\sharp}
and
\varphi^{\flat}
are
homeomorphisms. Relying on Lemma
14.1
, it
remains to show that
\varphi
is surjective.

Let
{(F_{i}^{-},F_{i}^{+})}_{i\in I}
be any element of
Z
. This means
that every
(F_{i}^{-},F_{i}^{+})
is in
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}}
, and
that for all
i\sqsubseteq j\in I
,

(F_{i}^{-},F_{i}^{+})=\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}(F_{j}^{-},F_{j}^{+})=(\mathbb{P}{p_{ij}}(F_{j}^{-}),\mathbb{P}{p_{ij}}(F_{j}^{+}))
. In particular,

{(F_{i}^{-})}_{i\in I}
is in
Z^{\sharp}
, hence is equal to

\varphi^{\sharp}(F^{-})
for some
F^{-}\in\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
, and

{(F_{i}^{+})}_{i\in I}
is in
Z^{\flat}
, hence is equal to

\varphi^{\flat}(F^{+})
for some
F^{+}\in\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
.
Explicitly, this means that
\mathbb{P}{p_{i}}(F^{+})=F_{i}^{+}
and

\mathbb{P}{p_{i}}(F^{-})=F_{i}^{-}
for every
i\in I
, namely that for
every
h_{i}\in\mathcal{L}{X_{i}}
,

F^{\pm}(h_{i}\circ p_{i})=F_{i}^{\pm}(h_{i})
(with
\pm
equal to
-
or
to
+
).

We claim that
(F^{-},F^{+})
is in
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
. It
suffices to check Walley’s condition. For all
h,h^{\prime}\in\mathcal{L}X
,
we write
h_{i}
for the largest map in
\mathcal{L}{X_{i}}
such that
h_{i}\circ p_{i}\leq h
, for every
i\in I
, and similarly with
h^{\prime}_{i}
. Then:

\displaystyle F^{-}(h+h^{\prime})

\displaystyle=F^{-}(\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}(h_{i}\circ p_{i})+\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}(h^{\prime}_{i}\circ p_{i}))

Lemma
10.2
, item 5

\displaystyle=F^{-}(\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}(h_{i}+h^{\prime}_{i})\circ p_{i})

+
is Scott-continuous

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}F^{-}((h_{i}+h^{\prime}_{i})\circ p_{i})

F^{-}
is Scott-continuous

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}F_{i}^{-}(h_{i}+h^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}(F_{i}^{-}(h_{i})+F_{i}^{+}(h^{\prime}_{i}))

Walley’s condition on
(F_{i}^{-},F_{i}^{+})

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}F_{i}^{-}(h_{i})+\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}F_{i}^{+}(h^{\prime}_{i})

\displaystyle=\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}F^{-}(h_{i}\circ p_{i})+\sup\nolimits^{\scriptstyle\uparrow}_{i\in I}F^{+}(h^{\prime}_{i}\circ p_{i})

\displaystyle=F^{-}(h)+F^{+}(h^{\prime}),

by using the Scott-continuity of
F^{-}
and
F^{+}
, and
Lemma
10.2
, item 5. The inequality
F^{-}(h)+F^{+}(h^{\prime})\leq F^{+}(h+h^{\prime})
is proved similarly.

We have now found an element
(F^{-},F^{+})
of
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}X
such
that
\mathbb{P}{p_{i}}(F^{+})=F_{i}^{+}
and
\mathbb{P}{p_{i}}(F^{-})=F_{i}^{-}
for
every
i\in I
, hence such that
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{i}}(F^{-},F^{+})=(F_{i}^{-},F_{i}^{+})
for every
i\in I
. Hence
\varphi(F^{-},F^{+})={(F_{i}^{-},F_{i}^{+})}_{i\in I}
. ∎

We apply Theorem
14.2
and list conditions under
which
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
preserves projective limits (equivalently,

{\mathbf{V}}_{\bullet}
, by Theorem
11.4
, hence the conditions
of Theorem
4.3
), and under which
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}

also preserves projective limits; in other words, we appeal to
Theorem
11.4
and to Theorem
13.8
, and
we obtain the following.

Corollary 14.3

Let
{(p_{ij}\colon X_{j}\to X_{i})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
be a
projective system of topological spaces, with canonical projective
limit
X,{(p_{i})}_{i\in I}
. Let
\bullet
be nothing,
“
\leq 1
” or “
1
”. If:

1.

the projective system is an ep-system,

2.

or
I
has a countable cofinal subset and each
X_{i}
is
locally compact sober (and compact, if
\bullet
is “
1
”),

3.

or every
X_{i}
is
\odot
-consonant sober (and compact, if

\bullet
is “
1
”) and every
p_{ij}
is a proper map,

then

{(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{X_{i}})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
is a
projective system of topological spaces, and

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X,{(\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}{p_{i}})}_{i\in I}
is
its projective limit, up to homeomorphism.

15
The
\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}
functor

We will transport this result to the matching model of convex lenses
over spaces of continuous (subprobability, probability) valuations of

[
51
]
, through a suitable homeomorphism. However,
this will work in a restricted setting.

There is such a homeomorphism between

\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X
, the subspace of

\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X
of convex lenses, and

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}X
, for every space
X
such that
\mathcal{L}X
is
locally convex
and
has a almost open addition map

[
19
, Theorem 4.17]
. The latter property means that for
all open subsets
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{V}
of
\mathcal{L}X
,

\upc(\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{V})=\{f\in\mathcal{L}X\mid\exists g\in\mathcal{U},h\in\mathcal{V},f\geq g+h\}
is open. When
\bullet

is “
1
”, we also need to require
X
to be compact. The
homeomorphism is the restriction of a retraction

(r_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}\,X},s_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}\,X}^{\bullet})
, where

r_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}\,X}\colon\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X\to\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}X
maps every lens
L
to the fork

(h\mapsto\inf_{G\in L}G(h),h\mapsto\sup_{G\in L}G(h))
,
and

s_{{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}\,X}\colon\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}X\to\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X
maps
(F^{-},F^{+})
to

\{G\in\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X\mid F^{-}\leq G\leq F^{+}\}
. The
fact that it is a retraction is also predicated on the fact that

\mathcal{L}X
is locally convex, has an almost open addition map, and
that
X
is compact if
\bullet
is “
1
”

[
19
, Proposition 3.32]
. We only consider the
homeomorphisms, not the retractions, here.

Lemma 15.1

The transformations
r_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}
and
s_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
between

\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}
and
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
are

{\mathbf{K}}^{\bullet}
-relative natural, where
{\mathbf{K}}^{\bullet}
is the full subcategory of

\mathbf{Top}
consisting of spaces
X
such that
\mathcal{L}X
is locally
convex, with an almost open addition map, and such that
X
is
compact in case
\bullet
is “
1
”.

Proof 59

Since these transformations consist of mutually inverse
homeomorphisms, it suffices to show that
r_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}
is natural.
Lemma 4.6 of
[
19
]
states that:
(*)
for every lens

L\in\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X
(in particular for any
convex lens), for every
h\in\mathcal{L}X
,

\sup_{G\in\upc L}G(h)=\sup_{G\in L}G(h)
and

\inf_{G\in cl(L)}G(h)=\inf_{G\in L}G(h)
. The action of
the
\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}
functor on a morphism
g
maps any lens
L
to

\upc g[L]\cap cl(g[L])

[
51
, Proposition
4.33]
. Hence, for every continuous map

f\colon X\to Y
, for every

L\in\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}^{\bullet}X
,

r_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}(\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}f(L))=(h\mapsto\inf_{G\in\upc(\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L]))}G(h),\allowbreak h\mapsto\sup_{G\in cl(\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L]))}G(h))
. For every
h\in\mathcal{L}X
,

\displaystyle\inf_{G\in\upc(\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L]))}G(h)

\displaystyle=\inf_{G\in\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L])}G(h)

by
(*)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{G\in\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]}G(h)

\displaystyle\text{since }\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L])\subseteq\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]

\displaystyle=\inf_{G\in\mathbb{P}f[L]}G(h)

by
(*)

\displaystyle\geq\inf_{G\in\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L])}G(h)

\displaystyle\text{since }\mathbb{P}f[L]\subseteq\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L]),

so all those values are equal. In particular,

\inf_{G\in\upc(\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L]))}G(h)=\inf_{G\in\mathbb{P}f[L]}G(h)
. Similarly,

\sup_{G\in cl(\upc\mathbb{P}f[L]\cap cl(\mathbb{P}f[L]))}G(h)=\sup_{G\in\mathbb{P}f[L]}G(h)
. Then

\inf_{G\in\mathbb{P}f[L]}G(h)=\inf_{G^{\prime}\in L}G^{\prime}(h\circ f)
,
and

\sup_{G\in\mathbb{P}f[L]}G(h)=\sup_{G^{\prime}\in L}G^{\prime}(h\circ f)
,
so

r_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}(\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}f(L))=(h\mapsto\inf_{G^{\prime}\in L}G^{\prime}(h\circ f),h\mapsto\sup_{G^{\prime}\in L}G^{\prime}(h\circ f))
. We
compare this to

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}f(r_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}(L))=\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}f(h^{\prime}\mapsto\inf_{G^{\prime}\in L}G^{\prime}(h^{\prime}),h^{\prime}\mapsto\sup_{G^{\prime}\in L}G^{\prime}(h^{\prime}))
, and we find that those are equal. ∎

Let us write
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{co}}X
for the space
\mathcal{L}X
,
but with the compact-open topology instead of the Scott topology. The
compact-open topology is generated by open subsets

[Q>r]\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\{h\in\mathcal{L}X\mid\forall x\in Q,h(x)>r\}
,
where
Q
ranges over the compact saturated of
X
and
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
We can even restrict to basic open subsets
[Q>r]
where
Q
is
compact saturated, since
[Q>r]=[\upc Q>r]
.

Lemma 15.2

For every weakly Hausdorff, coherent space
X
, addition is almost
open on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{co}}X
, viz., for all open subsets
\mathcal{U}
and

\mathcal{V}
of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{co}}X
,
\upc(\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{V})
is
open in
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{co}}X
.

Proof 60

For every compact saturated subset
Q
of
X
, let

\langle Q\searrow r\rangle
be the function that maps every
element of
Q
to
r
, and all others to
0
. A
co-step
function
is a pointwise supremum of a finite family of such
functions
[
10
, Section 2]
. We also define a relation

\prec
on functions from
X
to
\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
by
f\prec g
if and only
if for every
x\in X
,
f(x)\ll g(x)
, where
\ll
is the
way-below relation on
\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
—namely,
r\ll s
if and only if

r=0
or
r<s
. Finally, we write
\mathop{\Uparrow}f
for
\{g\in\mathcal{L}X\mid f\prec g\}
.

We claim that the sets
\mathop{\Uparrow}f
form a base of the compact-open
topology on
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{co}}X
, where
f
ranges over the co-step
functions. Let

f\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\sup_{i=1}^{n}\langle Q_{i}\searrow r_{i}\rangle
, where each

Q_{i}
is compact saturated and each
r_{i}\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Without loss of
generality, we assume that
r_{i}>0
. Then, for every

g\in\mathcal{L}X
,
f\prec g
if and only if

g\in\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}[Q_{i}>r_{i}]
. Indeed, if
f\prec g
, then
for every
i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, for every
x\in Q_{i}
,

f(x)\geq r_{i}
and
f(x)\ll g(x)
, so
g(x)>r_{i}
, using the
fact that
r_{i}>0
. Conversely, if

g\in\bigcap_{i=1}^{n}[Q_{i}>r_{i}]
, then for every
x\in X
, let

I\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\{i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}\mid x\in Q_{i}\}
. If
I
is
empty, then
f(x)=0\ll g(x)
. Otherwise, let
i\in I
be such
that
r_{i}
is largest. Then
f(x)=r_{i}
, and since

g\in[Q_{i}>r_{i}]
, we have
f(x)<g(x)
; in any case,

f\prec g
.

Any co-step function
f
takes only finitely many values, and

f^{-1}([r,\infty])
is compact saturated for every

r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}
. Conversely, if
f
is any function from

X
to
\overline{\mathbb{R}}_{+}
that takes only finitely many values and is such
that
f^{-1}([r,\infty])
is compact saturated for every

r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}
, then we claim that
f
is a co-step
function. Indeed, it suffices to list and sort the non-zero values
taken by
f
as
r_{1}>\cdots>r_{n}>0
, to define

Q_{i}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}f^{-1}([r_{i},\infty])
for every

i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, and to verify that

f=\sup_{i=1}^{n}\langle Q_{i}\searrow r_{i}\rangle
. In order to
see this, we lett

g\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\sup_{i=1}^{n}\langle Q_{i}\searrow r_{i}\rangle
, and we
show that
f^{-1}([r,\infty])=g^{-1}([r,\infty])
for every

r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}
. Both
f
and
g
take their values in the
same set
\{0,r_{1},\cdots,r_{n}\}
, so it is enough to verify that

f^{-1}([r_{i},\infty])=g^{-1}([r_{i},\infty])
for every

i\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
. But

g^{-1}([r_{i},\infty])=Q_{1}\cup\cdots\cup Q_{i}
, since

r_{i}>r_{i+1}>\cdots>r_{n}
; it is easy to see that, since

r_{1}>\cdots>r_{n}
, we also have

Q_{1}\subseteq\cdots\subseteq Q_{n}
, so

g^{-1}([r_{i},\infty])=Q_{i}=f^{-1}([r_{i},\infty])
.

It follows that the sum of any two co-step functions is a co-step
function. Indeed, if
f
and
g
are co-step functions, with values
taken in the finite sets
A
and
B
respectively, then
f+g
takes
its values in
A+B
, and for every
r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}
,

(f+g)^{-1}([r,\infty])=\bigcup_{a\in A,b\in B,a+b\geq r}(f^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap g^{-1}([b,\infty]))
. The latter is
compact saturated because the union is finite, and because

f^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap g^{-1}([b,\infty])
is compact
saturated. Indeed,
a+b\geq r>0
implies that
a
and
b
cannot
both be
0
. If
a=0
, then that is equal to

f^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap g^{-1}([b,\infty])=g^{-1}([b,\infty])
is compact saturated (since
b>0
); similarly similarly
if
b=0
; and if
a,b>0
, then

f^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap g^{-1}([b,\infty])
is compact
saturated because
X
is coherent.

Now let
f\in\upc(\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{V})
. There are two lower
semicontinuous map
g\in\mathcal{U}
and
h\in\mathcal{V}
such
that
f\geq g+h
. Since
g\in\mathcal{U}
, there is a co-step
function
g_{0}
such that
g\in\mathop{\Uparrow}g_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{U}
.
Similarly, there is a co-step function
h_{0}
such that

h\in\mathop{\Uparrow}h_{0}\subseteq\mathcal{V}
. Let

f_{0}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}g_{0}+h_{0}
:
f_{0}
is a co-step function, and

f_{0}=g_{0}+h_{0}\prec g+h\leq f
, so
f\in\mathop{\Uparrow}f_{0}
. It
remains to show that
\mathop{\Uparrow}f_{0}
is included in

\upc(\mathop{\Uparrow}g_{0}+\mathop{\Uparrow}h_{0})
, which will imply that it is
included in
\upc(\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{V})
.

Let
f^{\prime}
be any element of
\mathop{\Uparrow}f_{0}
. Let
A
(resp.
B
) be
the set of values taken by
g_{0}
(resp.
h_{0}
). We recall that

f_{0}^{-1}([r,\infty])=\bigcup_{a\in A,b\in B,a+b\geq r}(g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap h_{0}^{-1}([b,\infty]))
for every

r\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\smallsetminus\{0\}
. For every pair of values
a\in A
,

b\in B
such that
a+b>0
, for every

x\in g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap h_{0}^{-1}([b,\infty])
, we have

f_{0}(x)=g_{0}(x)+h_{0}(x)\geq a+b
, so
f^{\prime}(x)>a+b
. Since

g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap h_{0}^{-1}([b,\infty])
is compact,

\min_{x\in g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap h_{0}^{-1}([b,\infty])}f^{\prime}(x)
exists and is strictly larger than
a+b
. Hence it is also
strictly larger than
(1+\epsilon_{a,b})(a+b)
for some number

\epsilon_{a,b}>0
. Letting

\epsilon\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\min_{a\in A,b\in B,a+b>0}\epsilon_{a,b}
,
we have obtained that there is a number
\epsilon>0
such that for
all
a\in A
and
b\in B
such that
a+b>0
,

g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])\cap h_{0}^{-1}([b,\infty])\subseteq{f^{\prime}}^{-1}(](1+\epsilon)(a+b),\infty])
.

We claim that there are an open neighborhood
U_{a,b}
of

g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])
and an open neighborhood
V_{a,b}
of

h_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])
such that

U_{a,b}\cap V_{a,b}\subseteq{f^{\prime}}^{-1}(](1+\epsilon)(a+b),\infty])
. When
a,b>0
, this is because
X
is weakly
Hausdorff. If
a=0
, we simply take
U_{a,b}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}X
and

V_{a,b}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}{f^{\prime}}^{-1}(](1+\epsilon)(a+b),\infty])
, and
symmetrically if
b=0
.

For every
a\in A
, let

U_{a}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\bigcap_{b\in B,a+b>0}U_{a,b}
, and for every

b\in B
, let
V_{b}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\bigcap_{a\in A,a+b>0}V_{a,b}
. Those
are open sets, since the intersections are finite. For every

a\in A
,
g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])
is included in
U_{a}
, and
similarly for every
b\in B
,
h_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])
is included
in
V_{b}
. Additionally, for all
a\in A
and
b\in B
such that

a+b>0
,

U_{a}\cap V_{b}\subseteq U_{a,b}\cap V_{a,b}\subseteq{f^{\prime}}^{-1}(](1+\epsilon)(a+b),\infty])
.

Let
g^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\sup_{a\in A}(1+\epsilon)a\chi_{U_{a}}
and

h^{\prime}\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}\sup_{b\in B}(1+\epsilon)b\chi_{U_{b}}
. Those are
suprema of characteristic maps of lower semicontinuous maps, hence
are lower semicontinuous maps. Since

g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])\subseteq U_{a}
for every
a\in A
,

g_{0}\prec g^{\prime}
: for every
x\in X
, either
g_{0}(x)=0
or not, and
in the latter case, let
a\mathrel{\mathrel{\mathop{\kern 0.0pt=}\limits^{\text{def}}}}g_{0}(x)\in A\smallsetminus\{0\}
; then

x\in g_{0}^{-1}([a,\infty])
, so
x\in U_{a}
, and hence

g^{\prime}(x)\geq(1+\epsilon)a>a=g_{0}(x)
. Similarly,

h_{0}\prec h^{\prime}
. Finally,
g^{\prime}+h^{\prime}\leq f^{\prime}
: for every
x\in X
,
either
g^{\prime}(x)=h^{\prime}(x)=0
and this is clear, or

g^{\prime}(x)=(1+\epsilon)a
for some
a\in A
such that
x\in U_{a}
and

h^{\prime}(x)=(1+\epsilon)b
for some
b\in B
such that
x\in V_{b}
,
and
a+b>0
. Since
U_{a}\cap V_{b}\subseteq{f^{\prime}}^{-1}(](1+\epsilon)(a+b),\infty])
,
f^{\prime}(x)>(1+\epsilon)(a+b)=g^{\prime}(x)+h^{\prime}(x)
.

Hence
g^{\prime}\in\mathop{\Uparrow}g_{0}
,
h^{\prime}\in\mathop{\Uparrow}h_{0}
, and

f^{\prime}\geq g^{\prime}+h^{\prime}
. Therefore

f^{\prime}\in\upc(\mathop{\Uparrow}g_{0}+\mathop{\Uparrow}h_{0})
. Since
f^{\prime}
is
arbitrary in
\mathop{\Uparrow}f_{0}
,
\mathop{\Uparrow}f_{0}
is included in

\upc(\mathop{\Uparrow}g_{0}+\mathop{\Uparrow}h_{0})
, hence in

\upc(\mathcal{U}+\mathcal{V})
, as promised. ∎

Corollary 15.3

For every
\odot
-consonant, weakly Hausdorff, coherent space
X
,

\mathcal{L}X
is locally convex and addition is almost open on

\mathcal{L}X
.

Proof 61

The compact-open topology is always coarser than the Scott topology,
and coincides with it when
X
is
\odot
-consonant

[
6
, Proposition 13.4]
.
\mathcal{L}X
is locally
convex by
[
6
, Lemma 13.6]
, and almost openness
is by Lemma
15.2
. ∎

With all that, we transport the result of
Corollary
14.3
from forks to convex lenses over
spaces of continuous valuations as follows. The conditions are
stricter than what we are accustomed to, as we need the maps
p_{ij}

to be proper.

Theorem 15.4

Let
{(p_{ij}\colon X_{j}\to X_{i})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
be a
projective system of topological spaces, with canonical projective
limit
X,{(p_{i})}_{i\in I}
. Let
\bullet
be nothing,
“
\leq 1
” or “
1
”. If every
X_{i}
is
\odot
-consonant and
locally strongly sober (and compact, namely strongly sober, if

\bullet
is “
1
”) and if every
p_{ij}
is a proper map, then

{(\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}{\mathbf{V}}{p_{ij}}\colon\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}{\mathbf{V}}_{\bullet}{X_{j}}\to\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}{\mathbf{V}}_{\bullet}{X_{i}})}_{i\sqsubseteq j\in I}
is a projective system of topological spaces, and

\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}{\mathbf{V}}_{\bullet}X,{(\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}{\mathbf{V}}{p_{i}})}_{i\in I}
is its projective limit, up to homeomorphism.

Proof 62

We recall that the locally strongly sober spaces are exactly the
weakly Hausdorff, coherent, and sober spaces

[
22
, Theorem 3.5]
.

By Corollary
14.3
(case 3),
\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
preserves
limits of such projective systems. Lemma
15.1
gives
use a
{\mathbf{K}}^{\bullet}
-natural retraction (even isomorphism) of

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathtt{A}}{\mathtt{D}}{\mathtt{P}}}^{\bullet}
onto
\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}\mathbb{P}_{\mathtt{P}}
, or
equivalently onto
\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}_{\mathcal{V}}^{cvx}{\mathbf{V}}
, where
{\mathbf{K}}^{\bullet}
is the full
subcategory of
\mathbf{Top}
consisting of spaces
X
such that

\mathcal{L}X
is locally convex, with an almost open addition map, and
such that
X
is compact in case
\bullet
is “
1
”. We can then
apply Lemma
11.2
and conclude, provided we can
show that not only the spaces
X_{i}
are in
{\mathbf{K}}^{\bullet}
, but also
X
.
The spaces
X_{i}
are
\odot
-consonant, weakly Hausdorff and
coherent, so
\mathcal{L}{X_{i}}
is locally convex and addition is almost
open on it by Corollary
15.3
. The classes of
locally strongly sober spaces and of strongly sober spaces are
projective
[
24
, Theorem 5.1]
, so
X
is locally
strongly sober (and compact if
\bullet
is “
1
”), namely, weakly
Hausdorff, coherent, and sober (and compact if
\bullet
is
“
1
”). By Corollary
12.15
,
X
is also

\odot
-consonant. Therefore, by
Corollary
15.3
,
\mathcal{L}X
is also locally
convex with an almost open addition map. ∎

16
Conclusion

Looking back on what we did, it is apparent that we have dealt with
each functor at hand by its own specific techniques. It would be
nicer if there were a general projective limit preservation theorem
that would entail the results we have obtained. This is rather
unlikely: the conditions we have obtained for our results differ for
each functor we have considered, and those conditions were shown to be
necessary—at least in the first part of this paper
(Sections
3
—
9
).

The following are a few remaining open questions:

1.

Theorem
7.5
, item 3 requires each space
X_{i}
to
be locally compact sober, and we have shown that a similar result
would fail for spaces that are not completely Baire. Would the
conclusion of the theorem still hold if each
X_{i}
were assumed to
be quasi-Polish? domain-complete? LCS-complete?

2.

Theorem
8.8
states that projective limits of
sober spaces preserved by
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathsf{V}}
are preserved by
\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}^{\mathbf{A}}_{\mathsf{V}}
and

\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}^{\mathrm{q}}_{\mathsf{V}}
. Does the converse hold, namely is it true that projective
limits of sober spaces preserved by
\mathop{\mathcal{P}\ell}^{\mathrm{q}}_{\mathsf{V}}
are preserved by
{\mathcal{H}}_{\mathsf{V}}
?
We know that this is true in a special case
(Remark
8.10
), but we conjecture that this is
false in general.

3.

Corollary
12.15
states that a projective
limit of
\odot
-consonant sober spaces and proper bonding maps is

\odot
-consonant. Is it necessary that the bonding maps are proper
for this to hold? If not, then the conclusion of
Theorem
15.4
would also hold for limits of
projective systems with arbitrary continuous bonding maps (i.e., not
proper maps), provided that the index set has a countable cofinal
subset, and that each
X_{i}
is
\odot
-consonant and locally
strongly sober (and compact, namely strongly sober, if
\bullet
is
“
1
”); the proof would be the same, using case 2 of
Corollary
14.3
instead of case 3.

The author declares none.
